\int\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})=\mathbf{0},

when
\mathbf{y}\mid\mathbf{X}
has an elliptically contoured density
f_{\bm{\mu},\mathbf{\Sigma}}
with parameters
\bm{\mu}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P}
and

\mathbf{\Sigma}=\mathbf{V}_{P}
.
This means that
for each
j,s=1,\ldots,l
, we have

\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P})}{\partial\theta_{s}}=\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{\bm{\theta},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})}{\partial\theta_{s}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})=-\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})}{\partial\theta_{s}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})\right)

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}
is defined in (
7.8
).
As before, we find

\begin{split}\int\frac{\partial\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})}{\partial\theta_{s}}\,\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{s})=&-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{ku^{\prime}(d)}{2d}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{z}\cdot\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\,\Bigg|\,\mathbf{X}\right]\right]\\
&\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{v^{\prime}(d)}{2d}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{z}\cdot\mathbf{\Sigma}\,\Bigg|\,\mathbf{X}\right]\right]\\
&\qquad-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[v(d)\mathbf{L}_{s}\,\Bigg|\,\mathbf{X}\right]\right].\end{split}

(B.35)

The first term on the right hand side of (
B.35
) is equal to

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{ku^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)}{2\|\mathbf{z}\|}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right]\\
&\qquad=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{ku^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{3}}{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right].\end{split}

Furthermore, we can write

\begin{split}&\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right]\right)\\
&=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{j})^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\right]\\
&=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{j})^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right)^{T}\right]\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&=\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)^{T}\frac{1}{k(k+2)}\Big(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}+\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\Big)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right),\end{split}

using Lemma
B.4
.
Application of property (
B.32
) and the fact that
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{A})^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{B})=\text{tr}(\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B})
, yields

\begin{split}&\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{1/2}\right]\right)\\
&=\frac{1}{k(k+2)}\left(2\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})+\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\right).\end{split}

It follows that the first term on the right hand side of (
B.35
)
leads to a first term in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P})/\partial\theta_{s}
, which is equal to

\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{3}\right]}{2(k+2)}\Big(2\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})+\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\Big).

(B.36)

The second term on the right hand side of (
B.35
) is equal to

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{v^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)}{2\|\mathbf{z}\|}\mathbf{z}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{z}\mathbf{\Sigma}\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{v^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|}{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{u}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{v^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|}{2}\right]\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\frac{v^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|}{2}\right]\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbf{L}_{s}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\frac{1}{k}\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma}\\
&=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]}{2k}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\mathbf{\Sigma},\\
\end{split}

using Lemma
B.4
.
This leads to a second term in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P})/\partial\theta_{s}
, which is equal to

\begin{split}&-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]}{2k}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\\
&=-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]}{2k}\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right)\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\right).\end{split}

(B.37)

The third term on the right hand side of (
B.35
) leads to a third term
in
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P})/\partial\theta_{s}
, which is equal to

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{s})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s}\right).

(B.38)

We conclude that
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P})/\partial\theta_{s}
consists of three terms
given in (
B.36
), (
B.37
) and (
B.38
).
This means that
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta},j}(\bm{\xi}_{P})/\partial\theta_{s}

has a term
\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})

with coefficient

\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{3}\right]}{(k+2)}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]=\gamma_{1},

and a term
\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j})
with coefficient

\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{3}\right]}{2(k+2)}-\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]}{2k}=-\gamma_{2},

where
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
are defined in (
8.5
),
and where we use that
u^{\prime}(s)s^{3}=\rho^{\prime\prime}(s)s^{2}-\rho^{\prime}(s)s
and
v(s)=\rho^{\prime}(s)s-\rho(s)+b_{0}
.
Finally, from the definition of
\mathbf{L}
in (
7.9
) it follows that the
l\times l
matrix with entries

\begin{split}&\gamma_{1}\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})-\gamma_{2}\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{s})\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{L}_{j})\\
&=\gamma_{1}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{j})^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{s})\\
&\qquad-\gamma_{2}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{j})^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{s})\\
&=\gamma_{1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}-\gamma_{2}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{j})^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{s}),\end{split}

is the matrix

\gamma_{1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathbf{L}.

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma B.5
.

Suppose that
\rho
satisfies (R3)-(R4).
Let
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
defined in (
8.5
) and suppose that
\gamma_{1}>0
.
Then the inverse of
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))/\partial\bm{\theta}^{T}
exists and is given by

\begin{split}&a\Bigg(\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}\Bigg)^{-1}\\
&+b\Bigg(\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}\Bigg)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathbf{L}\Bigg(\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}\Bigg)^{-1}\end{split}

where
a=1/\gamma_{1}
and
b=\gamma_{2}/(\gamma_{1}(\gamma_{1}-k\gamma_{2}))
.

Proof.

Together with Lemma
2
, first write

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}^{T}}&=\gamma_{1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})^{T}\mathbf{L}\\
&\qquad=\gamma_{1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E},\end{split}

where

\mathbf{E}=\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathbf{L}
.
Since
\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}(P))=\mathbf{\Sigma}
,
by definition of
\mathbf{L}
, it follows that for
\bm{\theta}(P)=(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{l})^{T}
,

\mathbf{L}\,\bm{\theta}(P)=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\theta_{j}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{L}_{j})=\mathrm{vec}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{l}\theta_{j}\mathbf{L}_{j}\right)=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}).

This means that

\mathbf{E}\,\bm{\theta}(P)=\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathbf{L}\,\bm{\theta}(P)=\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k}).

(B.39)

Since
\mathbf{L}
has full rank, also
\mathbf{E}
has full rank.
This means that
(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}
exists, and satisfies

(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})=\bm{\theta}(P).

(B.40)

Now, write

\begin{split}&\gamma_{1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}\\
&=\Bigg(\gamma_{1}\mathbf{I}_{k}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\Bigg)(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}).\end{split}

When we multiply the first matrix from the left with a matrix of the same type,

\begin{split}&\Bigg(a\mathbf{I}_{k}+b\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\Bigg)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Bigg(\gamma_{1}\mathbf{I}_{k}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\Bigg),\end{split}

(B.41)

then we find four terms.
A term
\mathbf{I}_{k}
with coefficient
a\gamma_{1}
, two terms

\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1},

with coefficient
-a\gamma_{2}+b\gamma_{1}
, and the term

\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}

with coefficient
-b\gamma_{2}
.
Consider the scalar valued inner product in the middle

\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}\,\bm{\theta}(P)=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})=k,

(B.42)

by application of (
B.40
) and then (
B.39
).
It follows that the term with coefficient
-b\gamma_{2}
reduces to

k\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}.

Hence the matrix product in (
B.41
) is equal to

a\gamma_{1}\mathbf{I}_{k}+(-a\gamma_{2}+b\gamma_{1}-kb\gamma_{2})\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}

(B.43)

When we multiply the same matrix from the right,

\Bigg(\gamma_{1}\mathbf{I}_{k}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\Bigg)\Bigg(a\mathbf{I}_{k}+b\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\Bigg),

we find the same result (
B.43
).
This matrix is equal to
\mathbf{I}_{k}
if and only if

a\gamma_{1}=1
and
-a\gamma_{2}+b\gamma_{1}-kb\gamma_{2}=0
,
or equivalently

\begin{split}a&=1/\gamma_{1}\\
b&=\frac{a\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{1}-k\gamma_{2}}=\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{1}(\gamma_{1}-k\gamma_{2})},\end{split}

(B.44)

where we use that
\gamma_{1}>0
and

\gamma_{1}-k\gamma_{2}=\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]/2>0
,
due to (R3)-(R4).
We conclude that the inverse of

\gamma_{1}\mathbf{I}_{k}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}

exists and is equal to

a\mathbf{I}_{k}+b\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}

with
a
and
b
given in (
B.44
).
Hence, the inverse of the matrix

\gamma_{1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}

is equal to

\begin{split}&\Big(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}\Big)^{-1}\Bigg(\gamma_{1}\mathbf{I}_{k}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\Bigg)^{-1}\\
&=\Big(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}\Big)^{-1}\Bigg(a\mathbf{I}_{k}+b\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\Bigg)\\
&=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}.\end{split}

After inserting
\mathbf{E}=\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathbf{L}
,
this finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Corollary
5

Proof.

Since
\rho
is strictly increasing on
[0,c_{0}]
,
the function
u(s)=\rho^{\prime}(s)/s>0
, for
0<s\leq c_{0}
and zero for
s>c_{0}
.
This means that

\alpha=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\left(1-\frac{1}{k}\right)\frac{\rho^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)}{\|\mathbf{z}\|}+\frac{1}{k}\rho^{\prime\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]\geq\frac{1}{k}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho^{\prime\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\right]>0.

Furthermore, since
\mathbf{X}
has full rank, the inverse of
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]
exists.
It follows that the matrix
\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))/\partial\bm{\beta}
in (
8.3
)
is non-singular.
According to Lemma
B.5
, also

\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))/\partial\bm{\theta}
is non-singular.
Together, with Lemma
B.3
and Lemma
2
, we conclude that

\partial\Lambda/\partial\bm{\xi}
is continuously differentiable with a non-singular derivative at
\bm{\xi}(P)
,
so that Theorem
5
applies.
Together with Lemma
2
,
this implies that

\begin{split}\mathrm{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\beta},P)&=-\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\beta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\beta}}\right)^{-1}\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\xi}(P))\\
&=\frac{u(d_{0})}{\alpha}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]\right)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{0}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})\end{split}

where
d_{0}^{2}=(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})
.
From Theorem
5
, together with Lemma
2
, it also follows that

\begin{split}&\mathrm{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\theta},P)=-\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\xi}(P))\\
&=\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\times\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathrm{vec}\left(ku(d_{0})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}-v(d_{0})\mathbf{\Sigma}\right)\\
&=ku(d_{0})\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}\left((\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\right)\\
&\qquad-v(d_{0})\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma}).\end{split}

(B.45)

Consider the first term on the right hand side of (
B.45
).
We have that

\begin{split}&(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathrm{vec}\left((\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\right)\\
&\qquad=\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\end{split}

and from Lemma
B.5
,

\begin{split}&\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\\
&\quad=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T},\end{split}

(B.46)

where
\mathbf{E}=(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathbf{L}
.
This implies

\begin{split}&\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}\left((\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\right)\\
&\quad=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&\qquad+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\times\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right).\end{split}

The first term on the right hand side is equal to

\begin{split}&a\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L})\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\times\\
&\qquad\times\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\end{split}

and, with (
B.39
) and (
B.40
), the second term on the right hand side is equal to

\begin{split}&b\bm{\theta}(P)\bm{\theta}(P)^{T}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&=b\bm{\theta}(P)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&=b\bm{\theta}(P)\text{tr}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&=bd_{0}^{2}\bm{\theta}(P).\end{split}

It follows that the first term on the right hand side of (
B.45
) is equal to

\begin{split}&aku(d_{0})\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L})\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\times\\
&\quad\times\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&\qquad+bku(d_{0})d_{0}^{2}\bm{\theta}(P).\end{split}

Next consider the second term on the right hand side of (
B.45
).
We have that

(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})=\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k}),

and with (
B.46
),
together with (
B.39
) and (
B.40
),

\begin{split}&\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{\Sigma})\\
&\quad=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)\\
&\quad=a\bm{\theta}(P)+b\bm{\theta}(P)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)\\
&\quad=a\bm{\theta}(P)+b\bm{\theta}(P)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}\bm{\theta}(P)\\
&\quad=a\bm{\theta}(P)+b\bm{\theta}(P)\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\\
&\quad=(a+bk)\bm{\theta}(P).\end{split}

It follows that the second term on the right hand side of (
B.45
) is equal to

-v(d_{0})(a+bk)\bm{\theta}(P).

Putting things together, we find that
\mathrm{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\theta},P)
is equal to

\begin{split}&aku(d_{0})\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L})\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\times\\
&\quad\times\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\\
&\qquad+(bku(d_{0})d_{0}^{2}-av(d_{0})-bkv(d_{0}))\bm{\theta}(P).\end{split}

Since
v(d_{0})=u(d_{0})d_{0}^{2}-\rho(d_{0})+b_{0}
, we have that

\begin{split}bku(d_{0})d_{0}^{2}-av(d_{0})-bkv(d_{0})&=-\left(\frac{v(d_{0})}{\gamma_{1}}-\frac{k\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{1}(\gamma_{1}-k\gamma_{2})}(\rho(d_{0})-b_{0})\right)\\
&=-\frac{u(d_{0})d_{0}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}+\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}}+\frac{k\gamma_{2}}{\gamma_{1}(\gamma_{1}-k\gamma_{2})}\right)(\rho(d_{0})-b_{0})\\
&=-\frac{u(d_{0})d_{0}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}+\frac{\rho(d_{0})-b_{0}}{\gamma_{1}-k\gamma_{2}}\\
\end{split}

We conclude that
\mathrm{IF}(\mathbf{s}_{0},\bm{\theta},P)
is given by

\begin{split}&\frac{ku(d_{0})}{\gamma_{1}}\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1})\mathbf{L})\Big)^{-1}\mathbf{L}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})(\mathbf{y}_{0}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\\
&\qquad+\left(-\frac{u(d_{0})d_{0}^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}+\frac{\rho(d_{0})-b_{0}}{\gamma_{1}-k\gamma_{2}}\right)\bm{\theta}(P).\end{split}

This proves the corollary.
∎

B.5
Proofs of Section
9

As preparation we first establish the following lemma,
which is similar to Lemma 22 in
[
21
]
.

Lemma B.6
.

Let
\rho(\cdot)
be a real-valued function of bounded variation on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
The class of all functions on
\mathbb{R}^{p}
of the form

\mathbf{s}=(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X})\mapsto\rho(\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|)

with
\mathbf{A}
ranging over all
k\times k
matrices and
\bm{\beta}
ranging over
\mathbb{R}^{q}
,
has polynomial discrimination.

Proof.

Consider the class of functions

g_{\mathbf{A},\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},t)=\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|^{2}-\rho^{-1}(t)^{2}.

According to Lemma 18 in
[
21
]
,
it suffices to show that the functions
g_{\mathbf{A},\bm{\beta}}(\cdot,\cdot)
span a finite-dimensional vector space.
In order to do so, write

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|^{2}&=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}(\mathbf{A}^{T}\mathbf{A})_{ij}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})_{i}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})_{j}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{s=1}^{k}a_{is}a_{sj}\left(y_{i}-\sum_{r=1}^{q}x_{ir}\beta_{r}\right)\left(y_{j}-\sum_{w=1}^{q}x_{jw}\beta_{w}\right)\end{split}

This is a polynomial in
\mathbf{s}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k},x_{11},\ldots,x_{kq})
,
with coefficients in
\mathbb{R}
.
This means that the class of functions

g_{\mathbf{A},\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},t)=\|\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|^{2}-\rho^{-1}(t)^{2}
forms a finite dimensional vector space.
∎

A useful first step is the following lemma.

Lemma B.7
.

Let
u:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function of bounded variation.
Let
\mathbf{s}=(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X})=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{p})\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and define

g(\mathbf{s},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V})=u(\|\mathbf{V}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|)\quad\text{for }\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\mathbf{V}\in\text{PDS}(k).

Consider the classes of functions

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}&=\{g(\mathbf{s},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V}):\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\mathbf{V}\in\text{PDS}(k)\},\\
\mathcal{F}_{a}&=\{g(\mathbf{s},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V})s_{a}:g\in\mathcal{F}\},\\
\mathcal{F}_{ab}&=\{g(\mathbf{s},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V})s_{a}s_{b}:g\in\mathcal{F}\},\end{split}

for
a,b=1,\ldots,p
.
Denote by
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{G}_{a}
, and
\mathcal{G}_{ab}
,
the corresponding classes of graphs of the functions in

\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{F}_{a}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{ab}
, respectively.
Then
\mathcal{G}
,
\mathcal{G}_{a}
, and
\mathcal{G}_{ab}
, all have polynomial discrimination
for
a,b=1,\ldots,p
.

Proof.

Because the function
u(\cdot)
is of bounded variation, it follows from
Lemma
B.6
that the class
\mathcal{G}
has polynomial discrimination.
To show the same for the class
\mathcal{G}_{a}
, suppose that
\mathcal{G}_{a}
is not of polynomial discrimination.
This means that for every integer
N
there exists a set
V\subset\mathbb{R}^{p+1}
of
N
points,
such that all subsets of
V
can be written as
D_{a}\cap V
for some
D_{a}\in\mathcal{G}_{a}
.
Let
V=\{\mathbf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{N}\}
, where
\mathbf{v}_{i}=(\mathbf{s}_{i},t_{i})
, for
i=1,\ldots,N
.
Then for every
(\mathbf{s},t)\in V
with
\mathbf{s}=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{a},\ldots,s_{p})
,
it must hold that
s_{a}\neq 0
, otherwise this point cannot be separated from the other points
by an element of the class
\mathcal{G}_{a}
.
Define the set
V_{a}=\{(\mathbf{s},t/s_{a}):(\mathbf{s},t)\in V\}
.
Note that for
D_{a}\in\mathcal{G}_{a}
, we have

\begin{split}(\mathbf{s},t)\in D_{a}&\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad 0\leq t\leq g(\mathbf{s},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V})s_{a}\text{ or }g(\mathbf{s},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V})s_{a}\leq t\leq 0\\
&\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad 0\leq\frac{t}{s_{a}}\leq g(\mathbf{s},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V})\text{ or }g(\mathbf{s},\bm{\beta},\mathbf{V})\leq\frac{t}{s_{a}}\leq 0\\
&\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad(\mathbf{s},t/s_{a})\in D,\end{split}

where
D\in\mathcal{G}
.
This implies that every subset of
V_{a}
can be written as
D\cap V_{a}
, for some
D\in\mathcal{G}
.
However, this is in contradiction with the fact that
\mathcal{G}
has polynomial discrimination.
We conclude that also
\mathcal{G}_{a}
has polynomial discrimination.
A similar argument yields that
\mathcal{G}_{ab}
has polynomial discrimination.
∎

Lemma
B.7
is comparable to Lemma 3 in
[
18
]
, for similar classes of functions built from
functions
g(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{t},\mathbf{C})=u(\|\mathbf{C}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{t})\|)
, where
\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
\mathbf{C}\in\text{PDS}(k)
.
With Lemma
B.7
we can establish suitable bounds on the third term
of (
9.2
).
This is provided by the following key lemma.
Once having established Lemma
B.8
, asymptotic normality can be derived easily from (
9.2
).

Lemma B.8
.

Let
\Psi=(\Psi_{\bm{\beta}},\Psi_{\bm{\theta}})
be defined in (
7.5
)
and let
\bm{\xi}_{n}=\bm{\xi}(\mathbb{P}_{n})
and
\bm{\xi}_{P}=\bm{\xi}(P)
be the solutions to
minimization problems (
3.1
) and (
3.5
).
Suppose that
\rho
satisfies (R1)-(R4), such that
u(s)
is of bounded variation,
and suppose that
\mathbf{V}
satisfies (V4).
Suppose that
\bm{\xi}_{n}\to\bm{\xi}_{P}
, in probability, and that

\mathbb{E}\|\mathbf{s}\|^{2}<\infty
.
Then

\int\left(\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})-\Psi(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

(B.47)

Proof.

First write

\Psi_{\bm{\theta},j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi})=\Psi_{2,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi})-\Psi_{3,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi})
,
for
j=1,\ldots,l
, where

\begin{split}\Psi_{2,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi})&=u(d)(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{V}^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{j}\mathbf{V}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\\
\Psi_{3,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi})&=\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{V}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right)(\rho(d)-b_{0}),\end{split}

(B.48)

where
\mathbf{H}_{j}
and
d=d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi})
are defined in (
7.3
) and (
5.1
).
It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\int\left(\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})-\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}),

(B.49)

\displaystyle\int\left(\Psi_{2,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})-\Psi_{2,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}),

(B.50)

\displaystyle\int\left(\Psi_{3,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})-\Psi_{3,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}),

(B.51)

for
j=1,\ldots,l
.

To obtain (
B.51
),
first write

\mathbf{M}_{n}=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{n})^{-1}
and
\mathbf{M}_{P}=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{P})^{-1}
,
so that
\mathbf{M}_{n}\to\mathbf{M}_{P}
, in probability,
according to condition (V1).
Decompose as follows

\begin{split}&\Psi_{3,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})-\Psi_{3,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\\
&\qquad=\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{n}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{n})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right)(\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})))\\
&\qquad\qquad+\left\{\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{n}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{n})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right)-\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{P}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{P})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right)\right\}(\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))-b_{0}).\end{split}

(B.52)

After integration with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P
, the first term on the right hand side of (
B.52
)
becomes

\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{n}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{n})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right)\int(\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})))\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s}),

(B.53)

where with (V4),

\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{n}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{n})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right)\to\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbf{M}_{P}^{-1}\frac{\partial\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{P})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right)

(B.54)

in probability.
Furthermore, note that all functions

\rho(d(\cdot,\bm{\xi}))
, for
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathbb{R}^{l}
are members of the
class

\mathcal{F}=\left\{\rho(\|\mathbf{V}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|):\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\mathbf{V}\in\text{PDS}(k)\right\}.

From (R1)-(R2) it follows that the function
\rho
is of bounded variation (being the sum of two monotone functions).
According to Lemma
B.7
,
the class
\mathcal{G}
, consisting of subgraphs of functions in the class
\mathcal{F}
,
has polynomial discrimination.
Moreover, the class
\mathcal{F}
has a constant envelope.
Then as a result of the empirical process theory developed in Pollard
[
23
]

(e.g., see Theorem 1 in
[
18
]
), it follows that for every
\delta>0
,

\sup_{f_{1},f_{2}\in[\delta]}\sqrt{n}\left|\int\left(f_{1}(\mathbf{s})-f_{2}(\mathbf{s})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\right|\to 0

in probability, where

[\delta]=\left\{(f_{1},f_{2}):f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}\text{ and }\int(f_{1}-f_{2})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}P\leq\delta^{2}\right\}.

Because
\bm{\xi}_{n}\to\bm{\xi}_{P}
in probability one, the pair of functions
f_{1}(\mathbf{s})=\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})
and

f_{2}(\mathbf{s})=\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})
are in the set
[\delta]
,
for sufficiently large
n
, with probability tending to one.
It follows that

\begin{split}&\sqrt{n}\left|\int\left(\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})-\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{\mathbf{s}})\right|\\
&\quad\leq\sup_{f_{1},f_{2}\in[\delta]}\sqrt{n}\left|\int\left(f_{1}(\mathbf{s})-f_{2}(\mathbf{s})\right)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\right|\to 0,\end{split}

(B.55)

in probability.
Together with the fact that
\text{tr}(\mathbf{M}_{P}^{-1}\partial\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{P})/\partial\theta_{j})
is bounded,
this proves that (
B.53
) is of the order
o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
.
For the second term on the right hand side of (
B.52
), we have that according to the central limit theorem

\int(\rho(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))-b_{0})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{n}).

Together with (
B.54
) this implies that,
after integration with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P
,
the second term on the right hand side of (
B.52
) is of the order
o_{P}(1/\sqrt{n})
.
This proves (
B.51
).

To obtain (
B.49
),
decompose as follows

\begin{split}&\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})-\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\\
&\quad=\bigg\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{y}-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}\mathbf{y}\bigg\}\\
&\qquad-\bigg\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n}-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P}\bigg\}.\end{split}

(B.56)

We will treat both terms on the right hand side separately.
For the first term on the right hand side of (
B.56
),
we write

\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{y}+u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\mathbf{X}^{T}(\mathbf{M}_{n}-\mathbf{M}_{P})\mathbf{y}.

(B.57)

First consider the first term in (
B.57
).
We consider each single element of the vector
\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
separately.
For
i=1,\ldots,q
fixed, write

\left(\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{y}\right)_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}x_{ji}\left(\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{y}\right)_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}x_{ji}\sum_{s=1}^{k}m_{n,js}y_{s}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{s=1}^{k}x_{ji}y_{s}m_{n,js}.

Hence, after integration with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P
,
the
i
-th component of the first term in (
B.57
)
can be written as a finite sum with summands

\int\Big(u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big)x_{ji}y_{s}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})m_{n,js},

for
i=1,\ldots,q
and
j,s\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
fixed, where
m_{n,js}\to m_{P,js}
,
Because
u
is of bounded variation and since

\mathbf{s}=(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{X})=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{k},x_{11},\ldots,x_{kq})
,
all functions

u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}))x_{ji}y_{s}
, for
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathbb{R}^{l}
, are members of the class

\mathcal{F}_{ab}=\left\{u(\|\mathbf{V}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|)s_{a}s_{b}:\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\mathbf{V}\in\text{PDS}(k)\right\}.

According to Lemma
B.7
, the corresponding class of subgraphs has polynomial discrimination,
so similar to (
B.55
) it follows that
for each
i=1,\ldots,q
and
j,s\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
fixed,

\int\Big(u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big)x_{ji}y_{s}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

which means that

\int\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}\mathbf{y}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

(B.58)

Next, consider the second term on the right hand side of (
B.57
).
Similar to the first term,
after integration with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P
,
the
i
-th component of the second term on the right hand side of (
B.57
)
can be written as a finite sum
of summands

\int u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))x_{ji}y_{s}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})(m_{n,js}-m_{P,js}),

for
i=1,\ldots,q
, and
j,s\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
fixed.
According to the central limit theorem, the integral is of the order
O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
,
and since
m_{n,js}\to m_{P,js}
, in probability,
it follows that the product is of the order
o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.
We conclude that

\int u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\mathbf{X}^{T}(\mathbf{M}_{n}-\mathbf{M}_{P})\mathbf{y}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

(B.59)

Putting together (
B.58
) and (
B.59
),
it follows for the first term on the right hand side of (
B.56
) that

\int\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{y}-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}\mathbf{y}\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

(B.60)

For the second term on the right hand side of (
B.56
),
we write

\begin{split}&u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n}-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P}\\
&=\Big(u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n}\\
&\qquad+u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\Big(\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n}-\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P}\Big).\end{split}

(B.61)

Consider the first term on the right hand side of (
B.61
).
For
i=1,\ldots,q
fixed, write

\begin{split}\left(\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n}\right)_{i}&=\sum_{j=1}^{k}x_{ji}(\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}x_{ji}\sum_{s=1}^{k}m_{n,js}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})_{s}\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{k}x_{ji}\sum_{s=1}^{k}m_{n,js}\sum_{t=1}^{k}x_{st}\bm{\beta}_{n,t}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{s=1}^{k}\sum_{t=1}^{k}x_{ji}x_{st}m_{n,js}\bm{\beta}_{n,t}.\end{split}

We see that, after integration with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P
, the
i
-th component of the first term
on the right hand side of (
B.61
) can be written as a finite summation of summands

\int\Big(u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big)x_{ji}x_{st}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})m_{n,js}\bm{\beta}_{n,t},

for
i=1,\ldots,q
and
s,t,j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, where
m_{n,js}\to m_{P,js}
and

\bm{\beta}_{n,t}\to\bm{\beta}_{P,t}
, in probability.
All functions

u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}))x_{ji}y_{s}
, for
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathbb{R}^{l}
, are members of the class

\mathcal{F}_{ab}=\left\{u(\|\mathbf{V}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|)s_{a}s_{b}:\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\mathbf{V}\in\text{PDS}(k)\right\},

and
u
is of bounded variation.
According to Lemma
B.7
, the corresponding class of subgraphs has polynomial discrimination,
so similar to (
B.55
) it follows that

\int\Big(u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big)x_{ji}x_{st}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

which means that

\int\Big(u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big)\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

(B.62)

Next, consider the second term on the right hand side of (
B.61
).
We then have to deal with summands of the form

\int u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))x_{ji}x_{st}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})(m_{n,js}\bm{\beta}_{n,t}-m_{P,js}\bm{\beta}_{P,t}),

for
i=1,\ldots,q
, and
s,t,j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, where
m_{n,js}\to m_{P,js}
and

\bm{\beta}_{n,t}\to\bm{\beta}_{P,t}
, in probability.
According to the central limit theorem, the integral is of the order
O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.
Because,
m_{n,is}\bm{\beta}_{n,t}\to m_{P,is}\bm{\beta}_{P,t}
, in probability,
it follows that the product is of the order
o_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.
We conclude,

\int u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\Big(\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n}-\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

(B.63)

Putting together (
B.60
) and (
B.63
),
proves (
B.49
)

Finally, consider
\Psi_{2,j}
in (
B.48
), with
\mathbf{H}_{j}
defined (
7.3
).
Write

\begin{split}\mathbf{M}_{n}&=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{n})^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{n})\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{n})^{-1}\\
\mathbf{M}_{P}&=\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{P})^{-1}\mathbf{H}_{j}(\bm{\theta}_{P})\mathbf{V}(\bm{\theta}_{P})^{-1},\end{split}

so that
\mathbf{M}_{n}\to\mathbf{M}_{P}
, in probability,
according to condition (V4).
Decompose
\Psi_{2,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n})-\Psi_{2,j}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})
as follows

\begin{split}&\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})\\
&\qquad+u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\{(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})-(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P})\Big\}.\end{split}

(B.64)

The first term in (
B.64
),
can be written as the trace of the matrix

\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n},

where
\bm{\beta}_{n}\to\bm{\beta}_{P}
and
\mathbf{M}_{n}\to\mathbf{M}_{P}
, in probability.
As before, we consider each single entry of this
k\times k
matrix.
The
(i,j)
-th element of
(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}
is equal to

\begin{split}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})_{i}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})_{j}=y_{i}y_{j}&-\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{j}x_{is}\beta_{n,s}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}y_{i}x_{jt}\beta_{n,t}\\
&+\sum_{s=1}^{k}\sum_{t=1}^{k}x_{is}x_{jt}\beta_{n,s}\beta_{n,t}.\end{split}

(B.65)

We see that
the
(i,j)
-th entry of

\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}

is a combination of four summations.
The last of these summations arising from (
B.65
),
after integration with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P
, has summands

\int\Big(u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big)x_{is}x_{jt}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\,\beta_{n,s}\beta_{n,t},

where
\beta_{n,s}\beta_{n,t}\to\beta_{P,s}\beta_{P,t}
, in probability.
All functions

u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}))x_{ji}y_{s}
, for
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathbb{R}^{l}
, are members of the class

\mathcal{F}_{ab}=\left\{u(\|\mathbf{V}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|)s_{a}s_{b}:\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\mathbf{V}\in\text{PDS}(k)\right\},

where
u
is of bounded variation
According to Lemma
B.7
, the corresponding class of subgraphs has polynomial discrimination,
so similar to (
B.55
) it follows that

\int\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}x_{is}x_{jt}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

The other three summations that arise from the right hand side of (
B.65
) can be handled in the same way.
It follows, that for each
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
fixed,

\int\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})_{i}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}),

which means that

\int\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

After taking traces, we conclude that

\int\Big\{u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{n}))-u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

(B.66)

Next, consider the second term on the right hand side of (
B.64
).
First, note that

\begin{split}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})^{T}\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})&=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}y_{i}y_{j}m_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}y_{i}\sum_{s=1}^{k}x_{js}\beta_{s}m_{ij}\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}y_{j}\sum_{t=1}^{k}x_{it}\beta_{t}m_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{s=1}^{k}\sum_{t=1}^{k}x_{is}x_{jt}\beta_{s}\beta_{t}m_{ij}.\end{split}

This means that

\begin{split}&(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})-(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P})\\
&\quad=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}y_{i}y_{j}\left(m_{n,ij}-m_{P,ij}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{i}x_{js}\left(\beta_{n,s}m_{n,ij}-\beta_{P,s}m_{P,ij}\right)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{t=1}^{k}y_{j}x_{it}\left(\beta_{n,t}m_{n,ij}-\beta_{P,t}m_{P,ij}\right)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{s=1}^{k}\sum_{t=1}^{k}x_{is}x_{jt}\left(\beta_{n,s}\beta_{n,t}m_{n,ij}-\beta_{P,s}\beta_{P,t}m_{P,ij}\right).\end{split}

(B.67)

We see that the
(i,j)
-th entry of

u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\{(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})-(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P})\Big\}

can be written as the combination of four summations.
The last of the summations arising from (
B.67
),
after integration with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{n}-P
, has summands

\int u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))x_{is}x_{jt}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\left(\beta_{n,s}\beta_{n,t}m_{n,ij}-\beta_{P,s}\beta_{P,t}m_{P,ij}\right),

where
\beta_{n,s}\beta_{n,t}m_{n,ij}\to\beta_{P,s}\beta_{P,t}m_{P,ij}
, in probability.
According to the central limit theorem, the integral is of the order

O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
, whereas the second term tends to zero.
All functions

u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}))x_{is}x_{jt}
, for
\bm{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathbb{R}^{l}
, are members of the class

\mathcal{F}_{ab}=\left\{u(\|\mathbf{V}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta})\|)s_{a}s_{b}:\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\mathbf{V}\in\text{PDS}(k)\right\},

where
u
is of bounded variation
According to Lemma
B.7
, the corresponding class of subgraphs has polynomial discrimination,
so similar to (
B.55
) it follows that

\int u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))x_{is}x_{jt}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\left(\beta_{n,s}\beta_{n,t}m_{n,ij}-\beta_{P,s}\beta_{P,t}m_{P,ij}\right)=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).

The other three summations that arise from the right hand side of (
B.67
) can be handled in the same way, so that

\begin{split}&\int u(d(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P}))\Big\{(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{n}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{n})-(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P})^{T}\mathbf{M}_{P}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\beta}_{P})\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P)(\mathbf{s})\\
&\quad=o_{P}(n^{-1/2}).\end{split}

(B.68)

Putting together (
B.66
) and (
B.68
),
proves (
B.50
) for each
j=1,\ldots,l
.
This finishes the proof of Lemma
B.8
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
6

Proof.

As in the proof of Corollary
5
, it follows that

\partial\Lambda/\partial\bm{\xi}
is continuously differentiable with a non-singular derivative at
\bm{\xi}(P)
,
so that Theorem
6
applies.
According to Theorem
6
and Lemma
2
,
it follows that
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\beta}_{n}-\bm{\beta}(P))
is asymptotically normal with mean zero
and covariance matrix

\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})^{T}\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]\right)^{-1}

where
\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}
is defined in (
7.10
).
We find that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\Psi_{\bm{\beta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})^{T}\right]&=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[u(d)^{2}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\bm{\mu})(\mathbf{y}-\bm{\mu})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\Big|\mathbf{X}\right]\mathbf{X}\right],\end{split}

where
d^{2}=(\mathbf{y}-\bm{\mu})^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-\bm{\mu})
and

u(s)=\rho^{\prime}(s)/s
.
As before, with
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{y}-\bm{\mu})
and
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{z}/\|\mathbf{z}\|
,
according to Lemma
B.4
,
the inner conditional expectation can be written as

\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]}{k}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}.

This implies that the asymptotic covariance of
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\beta}_{n}-\bm{\beta}(P))
is given by

\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\right]}{k\alpha^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}^{T}\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{X}\right]\right)^{-1}.

Again, according to Theorem
6
and Lemma
2
,
it follows that
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\theta}_{n}-\bm{\theta}(P))
is asymptotically normal with mean zero
and covariance matrix

\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)^{T}\right]\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}

where
\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}
is defined in (
7.10
).
We have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)^{T}\right]\\
&=\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)^{T}\right]\mathbf{E}\end{split}

where
\Psi_{\mathbf{V}}
is defined in (
7.8
)
and

\mathbf{E}=\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathbf{L}

and

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)^{T}\right]\\
&=k^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{4}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right)^{T}\right]\\
&\quad-k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)v(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)^{T}\right]\\
&\quad-k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)v(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right)^{T}\right]\\
&\quad+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k}\right)^{T}\right].\end{split}

From Lemma
B.4
, the first term on the right hand side is equal to

\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{4}\right]}{k+2}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}+\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\right).

This leads to one term
\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}
with coefficient

\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{4}\right]}{k+2}

and using that, according to Lemma
B.4
,
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\mathbf{u}\mathbf{u}^{T}\right]=(1/k)\mathbf{I}_{k}
,
we find a second term
\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}
with coefficient

\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{4}\right]}{k+2}-2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)v(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[v(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\right].

Since
v(s)=u(s)s^{2}-\rho(s)+b_{0}
, we have that

\frac{k}{k+2}u(s)^{2}s^{4}-2u(s)v(s)s^{2}+v(s)^{2}=-\frac{2}{k+2}u(s)^{2}s^{4}+(\rho(s)-b_{0})^{2}.

This means that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)^{T}\right]\\
&=\delta_{1}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}\right)+\delta_{2}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\delta_{1}&=\frac{k\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{4}\right]}{k+2}\\
\delta_{2}&=-\frac{2}{k+2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{4}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\left(\rho(\|\mathbf{z}\|)-b_{0}\right)^{2}\right]\\
&=-\frac{2}{k}\delta_{1}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\left(\rho(\|\mathbf{z}\|)-b_{0}\right)^{2}\right]\end{split}

(B.69)

Since,
\mathbf{K}_{k,k}(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})=(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2})\mathbf{K}_{k,k}
,
together with (
B.32
),
this implies that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)^{T}\right]&=\mathbf{E}^{T}\left(\delta_{1}\left(\mathbf{I}_{k^{2}}+\mathbf{K}_{k,k}\right)+\delta_{2}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\right)\mathbf{E}\\
&=2\delta_{1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E}+\delta_{2}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}.\end{split}

Furthermore, according to Lemma
B.5
,

\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}=a(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}+b(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1},

with
a
and
b
defined in (
B.44
).
By application of (
B.39
), (
B.40
),
and (
B.42
), we find that

\begin{split}&\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)^{T}\right]\\
&=2a\delta_{1}\mathbf{I}_{k}+(a\delta_{2}+2b\delta_{1}+bk\delta_{2})(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)\mathrm{vec}\left(\Psi_{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{s},\bm{\xi}_{P})\right)^{T}\right]\left(\frac{\partial\Lambda_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{\xi}(P))}{\partial\bm{\theta}}\right)^{-1}\\
&=2\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}+\sigma_{2}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})\mathrm{vec}(\mathbf{I}_{k})^{T}\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}\\
&=2\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{E}^{T}\mathbf{E})^{-1}+\sigma_{2}\bm{\theta}(P)\bm{\theta}(P)^{T}\\
\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\sigma_{1}&=a^{2}\delta_{1}\\
\sigma_{2}&=2b(2a+kb)\delta_{1}+(a+kb)^{2}\delta_{2}.\end{split}

When we insert the expressions for
\delta_{1}
,
\delta_{2}
,
a
, and
b
given in (
B.69
) and (
B.44
),
then we find

\begin{split}\sigma_{1}&=\frac{k(k+2)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[u(\|\mathbf{z}\|)^{2}\|\mathbf{z}\|^{4}\right]}{\left(\mathbb{E}_{0,\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho^{\prime\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|^{2}+(k+1)\rho^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]\right)^{2}}\\
\sigma_{2}&=-\frac{2}{k}\sigma_{1}+\frac{4\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}[\left(\rho(\|\mathbf{z}\|)-b_{0}\right)^{2}]}{\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{k}}\left[\rho^{\prime}(\|\mathbf{z}\|)\|\mathbf{z}\|\right]\right)^{2}}\end{split}

By substituting
\mathbf{E}=\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right)\mathbf{L}
,
we find that the limiting covariance of
\sqrt{n}(\bm{\theta}_{n}-\bm{\theta}(P))
is given by

2\sigma_{1}\Big(\mathbf{L}^{T}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\otimes\mathbf{\Sigma}^{-1}\right)\mathbf{L}\Big)^{-1}+\sigma_{2}\bm{\theta}(P)\bm{\theta}(P)^{T}

This finishes the proof.
∎
